\section{Deferred proofs}



\subsection{Completeness and soundness of \cref{c:LinModFieldBin}}\label{a:LinModFieldBin}

\albertimportant{Review: AI written}

We prove \cref{t:thm_LinModFieldBits}. For readability, we temporarily denote   $\pi:\ZZ\to R$ for the reduction modulo $\Char(R)$ and we let
%
$$
\Relation_{\mathsf{gen}}\defeq \LinModFieldBin(\moduleonebound,\modulemorphism,\moduletwo,  \cC,L), \qquad \Relation_{\mathsf{core}}\defeq \LinBitsDual(\bits,\pi, R,\cC), \qquad \inp'\defeq (\mu,\uu),
$$
%
and $\PESAT=\PESAT(\comfield, Q_{\mathbb{B}\text{it}\ZZ},\cC, \pack)$, $\LIN = \LIN(\comfield, \cC, \pack)$ (as  in \cref{s:soundness_core_iop}). Recall from \cref{d:bitification} that a bitification of $\bff\in (\moduleonebound)^{\codedim_0}$ is any $\bfg\in \bits^{\codedim}$ with $\bff=L\cdot \bfg_S$. For such $\bfg$, we say that $((\mu,\vv),f,\bff)$ belongs to $\Relation_{\mathsf{gen}}^{\delta}$ \emph{via} $\bfg$ if $\hamming(f,\Enc_{\cC}\circ \pack\circ \pi_2(\bfg))<\delta$ and $\langle \psi(\bff),\vv\rangle=\mu$. Then  $((\mu,\vv),f,\bff)\in \Relation_{\mathsf{gen}}^{\delta}$ if and only if $((\mu,\vv),f,\bff)$ belongs to $\Relation_{\mathsf{gen}}^{\delta}$ via some bitification $\bfg$ of $\bff$. The honest prover uses $\bfg$ as witness in the call to \cref{c:core_iop}. The same applies to $\Relation_{\mathsf{gen}}$, with equality of words in place of the distance condition. Recall from \cref{d:bitification} that $L\cdot \bfg_S\in (\moduleonebound)^{\codedim_0}$ for all $\bfg\in \bits^{\codedim}$.


For $\bfg\in \bits^{\codedim}$ let $\bff_{\bfg}\defeq L\cdot \bfg_S\in (\moduleonebound)^{\codedim_0}$. By \cref{l:bitify}, $\langle \psi(\bff_{\bfg}), \vv\rangle = \langle \psi(\bfg_S), \psi(L)^{\mathsf{T}}\cdot \vv\rangle = \langle \pi(\bfg),\uu\rangle$, and hence
%
\begin{equation}\label{e:appendix_bitify}
(\inp', f, \bfg)\in \Relation_{\mathsf{core}}^{\delta} \quad \Longleftrightarrow\quad  ((\mu, \vv), f, \bff_{\bfg}) \text{ belongs to } \Relation_{\mathsf{gen}}^{\delta} \text{ via } \bfg,
\end{equation}
%
and likewise for $\Relation_{\mathsf{core}}$ and $\Relation_{\mathsf{gen}}$. Completeness then follows from \eqref{e:appendix_bitify} and \cref{t:thm_core_IOPP}.

For round-by-round knowledge soundness, we decompose \cref{c:LinModFieldBin} as in the proof of \cref{t:thm_core_IOPP}: the first IOR $\Pi_1'$ is the instance translation $(\mu,\vv)\mapsto \inp'$ followed by the IOR $\Pi_1$ of that proof (of \cref{a:OOD}, if Round 0 is executed), and the remaining IORs are $\Pi_\red$ and $\Pi_\iopp$ (their variants from \cref{a:OOD}, if Round 0 is executed). $\Pi_1'$ has the same messages and output as $\Pi_1$, and it is perfectly complete by \eqref{e:appendix_bitify}. By \cref{r:ior_compositoin} it suffices to show that $\Pi_1'$ has round-by-round knowledge soundness with relaxation $(\Relation_{\mathsf{gen}}^{\delta},\PESAT^{\delta})$ (resp.\ $(\Relation_{\mathsf{gen}}^{\delta},\PESAT^{\delta,\zeta,y})$) and with the same errors as $\Pi_1$; here $\kappa_0=\theta(\cP,\cM,\codedim)$ is computed with respect to the tensor decomposition $\uu=\uu^{(1)}\otimes\uu^{(2)}$ used in \cref{c:core_iop} (cf.\ \cref{r:tensor_dec_u}).

Let $\kstate_1$ be the knowledge state function of $\Pi_1$; recall that its extractor is the identity map. We set $\kstate(\inp,\oracle,\tr,\ww)=1$ if and only if $(\inp,\oracle,\ww)\in \Relation_{\mathsf{gen}}^{\delta}$ when $\tr$ contains no verifier message, and $\kstate(\inp,\oracle,\tr,\ww)\defeq \kstate_1(\inp',\oracle,\tr,\ww)$ otherwise; and we set $\rbrextractor(\inp,\oracle,\tr,\ww)\defeq \bff_{\ww}$ if $\tr$ ends with the first verifier message and $\ww\in \bits^{\codedim}$, and $\rbrextractor(\inp,\oracle,\tr,\ww)\defeq \ww$ otherwise. The conditions of \cref{def:knowledge-state-function} hold: at the empty transcript by construction, at prover moves because $\kstate$ is constant before the first verifier message and coincides with $\kstate_1$ afterwards, and at full transcripts because $\Pi_1'$ and $\Pi_1$ have the same output. At every verifier message after the first one, $\kstate$ coincides with $\kstate_1$ before and after the message and $\rbrextractor$ is the identity map, so the error of $\Pi_1'$ is that of $\Pi_1$. Finally, let $\rho$ be the first verifier message and $\tr$ the transcript preceding it, and suppose that $\kstate_1(\inp',\oracle,\tr\Vert\rho,\ww)=1$ and $\kstate(\inp,\oracle,\tr,\rbrextractor(\inp,\oracle,\tr\Vert\rho,\ww))=0$. Then $(\inp',\oracle,\ww)\notin \Relation_{\mathsf{core}}^{\delta}$: otherwise $\ww=\bfg\in\bits^{\codedim}$, $\rbrextractor$ outputs $\bff_{\bfg}$, and \eqref{e:appendix_bitify} gives $\kstate(\inp,\oracle,\tr,\bff_{\bfg})=1$. We distinguish three cases.
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*,nosep]
\item $\rho=(q,\pirand)$ (Round 1 executed, Round 0 not executed). By the definition of $\kstate_1$, $\ww=\bfg\in\bits^{\codedim}$, and $\kstate_1(\inp',\oracle,\tr,\bfg)=0$ by the above. This is precisely the event bounded by $\kappa_0$ in the proof of \cref{t:thm_core_IOPP}, so the error is at most $\kappa_0$.
\item $\rho$ is the empty message (Round 1 skipped). Then $\tr\Vert\rho$ is the full transcript, so $\ww=\bfg\in\bits^{\codedim}$, and, since appending the empty message does not change $\kstate_1$, the prover-move condition for $\kstate_1$ gives $\kstate_1(\inp',\oracle,\emptyset,\bfg)=1$, i.e.\ $(\inp',\oracle,\bfg)\in \Relation_{\mathsf{core}}^{\delta}$, a contradiction. The error is $0$.
\item $\rho=\zeta$ (Round 0 executed). By the definition of $\kstate_1$ in \cref{a:OOD}, $\mathsf{Coll}(f,\zeta)$ holds, which happens with probability at most $\kappa_{\mathsf{OOD}}$ over $\zeta$ by \cref{l:ood_collision}.
\end{itemize}


\subsection{Soundness of Out Of Domain Sampling (OOD) in \cref{c:core_iop}}\label{a:OOD}
\albertimportant{Review: AI written} 
When $\delta$ exceeds the unique decoding radius of $\cC$, Round 0 of \cref{c:core_iop} is executed and, as in \cite{stir,whir}, the OOD claim is carried along by the subsequent relations: $\PESAT^{\zeta,y}$ consists of the triples $((\inp, \zeta, y), \oracle, \wit)$ with $\zeta, y\in \comfield$, $(\inp,\oracle,\wit)\in \PESAT$ and $\mle{\pack(\wit)}(\bm{\zeta})=y$, where $\bm{\zeta}$ is the evaluation point of Round 0; $\LIN^{\zeta,y}$ is defined analogously, and $\PESAT^{\delta,\zeta,y}$, $\LIN^{\delta,\zeta,y}$ are obtained by replacing $\PESAT$, $\LIN$ with $\PESAT^\delta$, $\LIN^\delta$.

\begin{theorem}[\Cref{t:thm_core_IOPP} when Round 0 is executed]\label{t:thm_core_IOPP_OOD}
Let $\delta\in (0,1)$ and $L_\delta\geq 1$ be such that $\cC$ is $(\delta, L_\delta)$-list-decodable, let $\codedim/\comfieldexp$ be a power of two, and assume that Round 0 of \cref{c:core_iop} is executed and that the remaining hypotheses of \cref{t:thm_core_IOPP} hold, those on $\Pi_{\mathsf{red}}$ and $\Pi_{\iopp}$ also with $\PESAT, \LIN, \PESAT^{\delta}, \LIN^{\delta}$ replaced by $\PESAT^{\zeta,y}, \LIN^{\zeta,y}, \PESAT^{\delta,\zeta,y}, \LIN^{\delta,\zeta,y}$ (where $\Pi_{\mathsf{red}}$ copies $(\zeta,y)$ from its input instance to its output instance). Then the conclusion of \cref{t:thm_core_IOPP} holds with one more round and the additional first error term $\kappa_{\mathsf{OOD}} \leq \binom{L_\delta}{2}\cdot (\codedim/\comfieldexp-1)/|\comfield|$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{lemma}[OOD collision probability]\label{l:ood_collision}
Let $f\in \comfield^{\codelength}$, let $\mathsf{List}(f,\delta)$ be the set of $\bfg\in \FF_2^{\codedim}$ with $\hamming(f, \Enc_{\cC}\circ \pack(\bfg))<\delta$, and let $\mathsf{Coll}(f,\zeta)$ be the event that two distinct $\bfg, \bfg'\in \mathsf{List}(f,\delta)$ satisfy $\mle{\pack(\bfg)}(\bm{\zeta}) = \mle{\pack(\bfg')}(\bm{\zeta})$. Then
%
$$
\Pr_{\zeta\gets \comfield}[\mathsf{Coll}(f,\zeta)] \leq \binom{L_\delta}{2}\cdot \frac{\codedim/\comfieldexp-1}{|\comfield|}.
$$
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Since $\Enc_{\cC}\circ \pack$ is injective, $|\mathsf{List}(f,\delta)|\leq L_\delta$. For distinct $\bfg, \bfg'$ in the list, $P\defeq \mle{\pack(\bfg)}-\mle{\pack(\bfg')}$ is a nonzero multilinear polynomial in $r\defeq \log (\codedim/\comfieldexp)$ variables, and the substitution $X_i \gets \zeta^{2^{i-1}}$ maps its monomials $\prod_{i\in I} X_i$ injectively to the powers $\zeta^{\sum_{i\in I} 2^{i-1}}$, with exponents in $\range{0}{\codedim/\comfieldexp-1}$. Hence $P(\bm{\zeta})$ is a nonzero univariate polynomial in $\zeta$ of degree at most $\codedim/\comfieldexp-1$, and it vanishes for at most that many $\zeta$. A union bound over the $\binom{L_\delta}{2}$ pairs concludes.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{t:thm_core_IOPP_OOD}]
\albertimportant{Review: AI written} We modify the proof of \cref{t:thm_core_IOPP}. The IOR $\Pi_1$ now consists of Rounds 0, 1 and 2, and outputs $\left(\inp'=(g^{\bm{\gamma}}, g^{\bm{\mu}},\ \zeta,\ y),\ \oracle'= f, \ \wit'=\picomfield(\bff)\right)$: it is a $3$-round IOR from $\LinBitsDual$ to $\PESAT^{\zeta,y}$ whose first prover message and last verifier message are empty, and it is perfectly complete because the honest $y$ equals $\mle{\pack\circ\pi_2(\bff)}(\bm{\zeta})$. The IORs $\Pi_2$ and $\Pi_3$ are the variants of $\Pi_\red$ and $\Pi_\iopp$ for $\PESAT^{\zeta,y}$ and $\LIN^{\zeta,y}$ from the hypotheses; the round count and the composition are as before. It remains to show that $\Pi_1$ has round-by-round knowledge soundness with relaxation $(\LinBitsDual^{\delta}, \PESAT^{\delta,\zeta,y})$ and errors $(\kappa_{\mathsf{OOD}}, \kappa_0, 0)$.

Let $\kstate$ be the knowledge state function of the proof of \cref{t:thm_core_IOPP}, and for a transcript $\tr$ of $\Pi_1$ let $\bar{\tr}$ denote $\tr$ with the messages of Round 0 removed. Write $\mathsf{Ev}$ for the condition $\mle{\pack\circ\pi_2(\ww)}(\bm{\zeta})=y$ on $\ww$, and $\mathsf{Coll}$ for the event $\mathsf{Coll}(f,\zeta)$ of \cref{l:ood_collision}. We keep the identity extractor and define $\kstate'(\inp,\oracle,\tr,\ww)$ as $\kstate(\inp,\oracle,\bar{\tr},\ww)$ if $\tr$ is empty or $\tr=(\bot)$; as $\kstate(\inp,\oracle,\bar{\tr},\ww)\vee \mathsf{Coll}$ if $\tr=(\bot,\zeta)$; as $(\kstate(\inp,\oracle,\bar{\tr},\ww)\wedge \mathsf{Ev})\vee \mathsf{Coll}$ if $\tr$ ends with $(y,\mu')$ or with $(q,\pirand)$; and as $\kstate(\inp,\oracle,\bar{\tr},\ww)\wedge \mathsf{Ev}$ if $\tr$ contains $\bm{\mu}$. The conditions of \cref{def:knowledge-state-function} hold: at the empty transcript because $\bar{\tr}$ is empty, at full transcripts because $\PESAT^{\delta,\zeta,y}$ is $\PESAT^{\delta}$ together with $\mathsf{Ev}$, and at prover moves because $\kstate$ never increases at a prover move, adding the condition $\mathsf{Ev}$ only restricts, and $\mathsf{Coll}$ is never introduced by a prover message. Since $\kstate'$ can only change from $0$ to $1$ at the message $\zeta$ through $\mathsf{Coll}$, the error at $\zeta$ is at most $\Pr_{\zeta}[\mathsf{Coll}]\leq \kappa_{\mathsf{OOD}}$ by \cref{l:ood_collision}; the error at the empty last verifier message is $0$. For the error at $(q,\pirand)$: a witness $\ww$ with $\kstate'=0$ before this message and $\kstate'=1$ after it satisfies $\neg\mathsf{Coll}$, $\mathsf{Ev}$, $(\inp,\oracle,\ww)\notin \LinBitsDual^{\delta}$ and $\kstate(\inp,\oracle,(\mu',(q,\pirand)),\ww)=1$. In particular $\pi_2(\ww)\in \mathsf{List}(f,\delta)$ and, as $\mathsf{Coll}$ fails, it is the unique element of the list with evaluation $y$ at $\bm{\zeta}$; hence $\ww$, and with it the polynomial $h$ of the proof of \cref{t:thm_core_IOPP}, are determined by $(f,\zeta,y,\mu')$ before $(q,\pirand)$ is drawn, and the first statement of that proof bounds the probability of this event by $\kappa_0$. The case where Round 1 is skipped is handled as there.
\end{proof}




\section{An end-to-end \ftwoz-based SNARK}\label{s:to_snark}
% moved from the technical overview (former s:to_snark subsection), 2026-09-11




We present an example SNARK that uses \ftwoz\ as PCS. Throughout the exposition we make several simplifying assumptions, for the sake of clarity. We discuss the general cases in \cref{s:to_general_bitz}. We emphasize that the main novelty is the PCS (Step 4 below). The techniques surrounding the PCS can be traced back to \cite{zincplus,zaratan}. 
    

Let  $S$ be a finitely generated ring, for example $S$ may be the integers $\ZZ$, a prime field, a lattice ring, a finite field extension, etc. For simplicity, for now let $S$ be either $\ZZ$ or a finite field $\FF$ (possibly an extension field). We discuss the general case in \cref{s:to_general_bitz}. If $S=\ZZ$, then we assume a public parameter $B$ specifying the maximum bit-size of a witness element. We let $S_{\leq B}$ denote either the finite field $\FF$ or the integer range $\range{0}{2^B-1}$.

In a proof system over a ring $S$, given a public vector $\inp=\xx\in S^\inplen$, the prover wants to prove it knows $\wit=\bff\in S_{\leq B}^{\codedim_0}$ such that \begin{equation}\label{e:intro_eq_0}\pp_i(\xx,\bff)=0 \qquad \text{in  } S \qquad \text{ for all } i\in [m], \end{equation} where $\pp=(\pp_i)_{i\in [m]}$ is a public collection of polynomials over $S$.  For example, in an \emph{R1CS constraint}, $$\pp_i(\xx,\ww) = (A\cdot (\xx,\bff))_i \cdot (B\cdot (\xx,\bff))_i  - (C\cdot (\xx,\bff))_i$$
where $A,B,C$ are $m\times (\inplen + \codedim_0)$ matrices over $S$. 

%\bigskip



%\noindent \ftwoz \ proceeds as follows:

     \albertimportant{AI-drafted (Claude Opus 5), 2026-09-11 -- review before submission}
     \paragraph{Step 1: Commitment.} The prover commits to $\bff$ with \ftwoz, i.e.\ it sends a commitment to $\Enc_\cC\circ\pack\circ\pi_2(\bitstext(\bff))$ over $\comfield$, as in Step 1 of \cref{s:to_simple_bitz,s:to_general_rings}. This must happen before Step 2, since the soundness of fingerprinting relies on the witness being fixed before the verifier samples $q$.

     \paragraph{Step 2: Projection to a   finite field $\FF$.}  If $S=\ZZ$, we perform the fingerprinting step of \cite{zaratan}. Concretely, the verifier samples a random prime $q$  of $\approx \lambda$ bit size. Then the claim \eqref{e:intro_eq_0} is replaced by
\begin{equation}\label{e:intro_eq_05}\pi(\pp_i)(\pi(\xx),\pi(\bff))=0 \qquad \text{in  } \FF \qquad \text{ for all } i,\end{equation} 
where $\pi:\ZZ\to \FF$ denotes reduction modulo $q$, and $\pi(\pp_i)$ is $\pp_i$ with coefficients reduced modulo $q$. 

If $S=\FF$ is a finite field, this step is skipped (unless we want to use the approach from \cref{r:to_reducing_ring}). Note we abuse the notation and use the same symbol $\FF$ to denote either the field $\FF$ above or $S$ if $S$ is a field. In this latter case we also let $\pi=\mathsf{Id}: \FF \to \FF$ denote the identity homomorphism.

% In \cref{s:to_general_bitz} we discuss other rings and other projections.

%This step can be skipped if one already has an efficient PIOP for polynomial constraints over $R$; for example, if $R$ is a finite field of reasonable size. 

\renewcommand{\lift}{\mathsf{lift}}

\begin{remark}[Reducing the size of $S$, if necessary]\label{r:to_reducing_ring} If $S$ is a prime field of very large size, one may prefer to lift the initial polynomial constraints to $\ZZ$  first (between Step 1 and Step 2), and then project onto a random prime field $\FF$ of bit-size $\approx \lambda$ during the present Step 2. This can compress the bit-size of the witness in the next steps by a very large factor. 
    
    To \emph{lift the constraints to $\ZZ$}, the prover publishes the integer values $a_i\defeq \lift(\pp_i)(\lift(\xx),\lift(\bff))$  for all $i$, where $\lift$ is the natural lift from a prime field to integers. Then the initial constraints over $S$ are replaced by $\lift(\pp_i)(\lift(\xx),\lift(\bff))-a_i = 0$ in $\ZZ$.

If $S$ is a proper finite field extension (or any finitely generated ring), one can also lift constraints. This time, one follows an approach similar to Step 3 of \cref{s:to_general_rings}.

A scenario where this can be desirable is when proving lattice-type computations, where rings are sometimes very large \cite{ccks}.
\end{remark}

     \paragraph{Step 3: PIOP over $\FF$.} As in \cite{zaratan,zincplus,limber}, a PIOP is run over $\FF$, reducing \eqref{e:intro_eq_05} to a linear claim over $\FF$ or, if $\FF$ is too small, to a field extension  $\FF_{\mathsf{ext}(e)}$ of $\FF$.  The claim is  of the form
\begin{equation}\label{e:intro_eq_1}
\langle \vv, \pi(\bff)\rangle =\mu \qquad \text{in } \FF_{\mathsf{ext}(e)},
\end{equation}
for some $\mu\in\FF_{\mathsf{ext}(e)}$, $\vv\in (\FF_{\mathsf{ext}(e)})^{\codedim_0}$, where it is understood that $\FF_{\mathsf{ext}(e)}=\FF$ if the PIOP did not need to use a field extension. 

In general, if $\FF$ is a prime field of $\approx \lambda$ bits (e.g.\ if we started with $R=\ZZ$), then $\FF_{\mathsf{ext}}=\FF$. The field $\FF_{\mathsf{ext}}$ may be a proper extension if, for example, we started with $S$ being a small finite field,  and the PIOP had to use challenges in an extension of $\FF$ to guarantee soundness.
 
We  assume $\vv$ admits a tensor decomposition $\vv=\vv^{(1)} \otimes \vv^{(2)} \in \FF^{n_1 \cdot n_2}$. This is typically the case since $\vv$ is usually an ``eq'' vector, i.e.\ $\vv=(\eq(\bb,\rr))_{\bb\in \bits^{\log \codedim_0}}$ for some $\rr\in \FFext^{\log\codedim_0}$. This will be leveraged crucially, for efficiency purposes, in Steps 4 and 5 of \cref{s:to_simple_bitz}.

For simplicity, we assume $\FF_{\mathsf{ext}}=\FF$  and we assume $\FF$ is a prime field. We discuss the general case in \cref{s:to_general_bitz}. 

\albertimportant{AI-drafted (Claude Opus 5), 2026-09-11 -- review before submission}
\paragraph{Step 4: Opening with the \ftwoz\ PCS.} The claim \eqref{e:intro_eq_1} is a linear claim, over $\FF$, about the image of the witness committed in Step 1 under the homomorphism $\pi: S\to \FF$. That is, it is an instance of the relation $\LinModFieldBin(S,\FF,\pi)$ from \cref{s:to_general_rings} (or of $\LinModFieldBin(\FF_q)$ from \cref{s:to_simple_bitz} if $S=\FF=\FF_q$), with the commitment of Step 1 as oracle, and prover and verifier prove it with the \ftwoz\ PCS.

\albertimportant{Link to \ftwoz\ PCS}

   
\subsection{Other rings}\label{s:to_general_bitz}
\albertimportant{AI-drafted (Claude Opus 5), 2026-09-11 -- review before submission}
Since the \ftwoz\ PCS commits over any finitely generated ring $S$ (\cref{s:to_general_rings}), the pipeline above extends to constraints over rings such as $\ZZ[X]/(h(X))$ or $\FF_q[X]/(h(X))$ for a monic $h(X)$ (e.g.\ the cyclotomic rings of lattice-based cryptography): its Step 2 is replaced by the generalized fingerprinting of Zinc+ \cite{zincplus}, which projects polynomial constraints over such rings, together with their ideal membership constraints, onto a random prime field through a random ring morphism (much like Step 3 of \cref{s:to_general_rings}), while its Steps 1, 3 and 4 are unchanged. Likewise, if the PIOP outputs its claim over an extension field $\FFext$ of $\FF$, Step 4 simply runs the \ftwoz\ PCS with $R=\FFext$, and Step 3 of \cref{s:to_general_rings} projects the claim back to a random ``small'' prime field. We do not formalize these extensions in this work; the resulting constraint system is a subclass of the UCS relation of \cite{zincplus} \albert{Mention we could allow for different characteristics in the same relation?}, and implementing and benchmarking lattice-type computations (e.g.\ Falcon signature verification) with \ftwoz\ is left as future work.
